| 146 | **BUG (product quality / honest numbers, planner share surface): the post-waitlist share path is subscription-hardcoded, number-wrong and link-less for TARGET/GROWTH plans.** `mixStats()` (planner.js ~285) resolved ANY `GOALS[]` entry with a numeric `target`, but that field means `$/mo` for a SUBSCRIPTION goal and the **item price** for a TARGET goal (`iphone` 1100, `watches` 400, `sneakers` 180, none carrying `isMonthly`) — so an iPhone was read as a $1,100/mo subscription and `foreverNumber()` turned it into ~$155k-$220k of capital the user never entered. The mix-seed effect (~1684) ran for EVERY archetype, so that bogus bundle existed from first render. WHAT SHIPPED, all in `test_audit_prescan.js` (+259/−1, **zero product-file lines**): `stripJsCommentsAndStrings()` gains regex-literal state via a new `isRegexPosition()` (previous-significant-emitted-char heuristic, `REGEX_PREV_PUNCT`/`REGEX_PREV_KEYWORDS`), honouring `\` escapes and `[`…`]` classes in which `/` does not terminate, then dropping the literal like a string; a frozen verbatim copy of 185's pre-fix scanner as `legacyStrip()` that the tests assert stays **silently green at 3** on the same mutation the fixed scanner counts **4** (without it the new tests could pass for reasons unrelated to the fix); `deriveTailMarker()` + a Leg B invariant that the artifact's own final executable line (`process.exit(unresolved ? 3 : 2)`, `audit-app.js:3201`, read at run time and asserted present in the raw source first so it cannot rot) survives stripping — one assertion that catches **any** future tail-swallowing shape, not only the two measured. **A BUG THE BUILD AGENT FOUND IN ITS OWN FIX MID-BUILD, kept because the reasoning is a fact about JavaScript rather than a hack:** `//` and `/*` must fall through to the comment branches *unconditionally* — an empty regex body and a bare leading `*` quantifier are both invalid JS, so neither can legally OPEN a regex; without that guard every `//` comment following a `;` or `}` parsed as an empty regex and corrupted the whole file. An unterminated regex bails at the newline, so a heuristic misfire costs at most one line and can never run to EOF — the exact failure mode this item removes. **A6b's assertion is byte-unchanged at `occurrences === 3`** — the count not moving is the evidence the gate did not get looser. DEVIATION, disclosed by the builder and **reproduced rather than accepted** by the verifier: the mutation fixtures inject before the tail marker, not "before the existing call sites" as criterion 2's prose says, because `audit-app.js`'s last literal `*/` is at offset 171,689 with all three real call sites at 156,628–170,081 — an earlier injection lets `legacyStrip` recover at that `*/` and return **4**, the same answer as the fixed scanner, making criterion 3 **unfalsifiable**. The honest consequence is stated, not glossed: "swallows the rest of the file" holds only when no `*/` follows; where one does, the damage is bounded to that span (which is exactly what `:324`/`:771`/`:811` do). NOT PROVEN, disclosed: not a real parser (post-increment-then-regex unhandled, absent from this repo); and the verifier's own sharpest finding — under a heuristic forced to treat every `/` as a regex start, the real file's count still came out 3 and only the explicit synthetic division test caught it, so the division direction's safety net is that test, not the real-file count. TESTS: `test_audit_prescan.js` **43/0 → 48/0** (A6b count still exactly 3), plain lane **39/39** exact baseline match, wall time **1m43.2s** inside the 5-min box, `git status --porcelain` clean after every mutation run (no stray fixtures, nothing under `tokens/`/`chains/`). | 187 | **The entire design system is held hostage by an unused third-party font CDN.** `style.css:1` is `@import url(https://api.fontshare.com/…satoshi…)`; every page loads `style.min.css` with the deferred pattern `media="print" onload="this.media='all'"`; a `<link>`'s `load` event does not fire until its `@import`ed sub-resources resolve — so when fontshare is blocked (ad-blocker, content blocker, corporate/regional filtering, CDN outage) the swap never runs and all **545 rules** of the design system stay applied to **print media only**. The page renders completely unstyled — no cards, no chips, no layout, no neumorphic tokens, no dark mode. Measured this run on **`/plan.html`, `/`, `/home.html?token=USDC` and `/home.html?pool=…` — 0 of 6 loads swapped**, `--color-background` empty on all of them, while `planner-styles.min.css`/`pool-detail-styles.min.css`/`landing-styles.css` (no `@import`) swapped every time. Cause proven by controlled substitution in a 5-line control file, not inferred: strip line 1 and the same sheet swaps to `all` and the token resolves `#E2E8F0`. **The imported font is never used** — `--font-family-base` (`style.css:93`) is `"FKGroteskNeue","Geist","Inter",…`, the real font is self-hosted at `style.css:783`, and `grep -i satoshi *.css` returns only the `@import` itself. So the request that can break every page fetches a typeface the product never renders; the fix is a deletion with no visual change on the happy path. Hits `?pool=` (the north-star surface), `?token=` (the SEO lifeline), `/plan.html` and `/`. Prod ships the identical markup (`curl` confirmed). Fix is a **2-line deletion** (`style.css:1` + its blank line) plus a script-regenerated `style.min.css` — the imported Satoshi face is referenced by **zero** `font-family` declarations, so there is no happy-path visual change; verifier programmatically confirmed the minified file’s post-`@import` content is byte-for-byte identical and that the committed artifact matches a fresh `node minify-assets.js` (md5 `836d9648…`). | 211 | **`yield.garden` serves the whole app on a second domain - consolidate it with a 301.** Measured live: `www.yield.garden` returns **200** and serves the full app plus the full static estate. **The exposure is far smaller than it looks and the reasons are recorded so nobody panics:** the 2,154-page static estate **already self-canonicalises correctly** (`www.yield.garden/tokens/usdc` emits `<link rel="canonical" href="https://www.defi.garden/tokens/usdc">`), and `robots.txt` + every sitemap served from that host already point at `www.defi.garden`, so it submits no URLs of its own. **The real gap is one page**: `home.html` has a static `og:url` (`:29`) but **no static canonical** - it is injected client-side by `canonical.js` (`SITE_ROOT` hardcoded to defi.garden, `:29`), so it only lands on Google's deferred JS render pass. That is exactly what GSC reports: `Duplicate without user-selected canonical` = **1 page**, against **26 impressions / 0 clicks / 16 months**. **Premise corrected, and it sets the score:** Google does not *penalise* cross-domain duplicates - it picks one URL and consolidates. The real costs are wrong-origin selection, split link/engagement signals, and doubled crawl budget on a daily-regenerated estate. Real, worth removing, **not an emergency**. Fix: host-conditioned permanent redirect in `vercel.json` preserving path AND query, plus a static canonical in `home.html` as the no-JS floor. KEEP the domain and its GSC property - verification is how the redirect gets monitored. **Not on the NEVER list** (nothing de-indexed; traffic consolidated onto the canonical origin is the opposite of de-indexing) but HIGH tier: `vercel.json` is config/infra and a host-predicate mistake in a `/(.*)` redirect is a whole-site outage. The one assertion that matters: `canonical.js` must still override the new static tag on `?token=`/`?pool=` views, or thousands of distinct canonicals collapse to root | 6.0 | SHIPPED (2026-08-03, ONE commit on `claude/loop-211` + PR, merged by this run after verifier **PASS tier HIGH**, independently assigned — verifier executed the host regex and `path-to-regexp@6` itself, built 10 mutants against the new guard and confirmed every one goes red, and re-derived the leg-2 contradiction from `test_canonical.js:112` + `specs/011.md` rather than trusting the notes). **LEG 1 ONLY — leg 2 (a static `<link rel="canonical">` in `home.html`) deliberately NOT built and the verifier agreed it should not be:** spec 211 is self-contradictory (§Change item 2 requires the tag; §Acceptance requires `test_canonical*.js` pass unmodified, and that test asserts the tag must not exist — a shipped guard from 011, which measured the damage it caused). Leg 2 is also subsumed — after a 301 that host serves no HTML to carry a canonical. Tests: 128/135 in-suite, the 7 reds all EADDRINUSE port collisions from a concurrent verifier run, **all 7 re-run serially and green**; `test_canonical.js` byte-unmodified 24/24. **The redirect itself is unverifiable pre-merge** (`dev-server.js` never reads `vercel.json`; a preview deploys on a host the predicate deliberately cannot match) — post-merge live curl recorded in `specs/211-notes.md`. | 212 | **Every parameterized URL returns the identical generic markdown - the agent asks about one pool and gets the homepage index.** Prompted by the Resend agent-readability pattern; **this repo is already well past most of that checklist**, so this is the residue, not the checklist. VERIFIED WORKING, do not rebuild: `llms.txt` (200, 6,641 B, proper `##` headings, 9 `?pool=` links), `llms-full.txt` (205,672 B), `.well-known/api-catalog` (200), `.well-known/mcp/server-card.json` (200), `.well-known/agent-skills/index.json` (200), `Link:` headers on every response, the 2,154-page estate genuinely server-rendered, daily regen keeping them in sync. THE DEFECT: `vercel.json`'s markdown rewrite is `source: "/"`, and a query string does not change the matched path - so `/`, `/?token=USDC` and `/?pool=43641cf5-...` all return **byte-identical** site-index markdown, while `/tokens/usdc` and `/chains/solana` return **`<!DOCTYPE html>`** (18,814 B, no markdown at all) and `/plan.html` returns `Redirecting...`. **Worse than returning nothing:** an agent that asks for one pool and gets a well-formed plausible site index has no signal it got the wrong document, and answers from it - the exact failure that makes an agent recommend whoever it could parse. Compounding: the one genuinely agent-readable surface (the estate) has no markdown twin, so agents parse HTML where markdown would work and get wrong-markdown where no facts exist. FIX: generate `.md` twins in the SAME generator run as the HTML (sync by construction, not by discipline), make negotiation path-aware, and **remove the silent generic fallback** - no twin means serve the HTML, never `llms.txt`. That last leg is cheap, independently valuable, and ships even if the rest is cut. **Scored as a capability bet, NOT measured demand: there is no measured agent traffic today and an agent that never runs JS cannot fire a client-side event - structurally unmeasurable with current instrumentation, stated plainly rather than given an invented metric** | 7.5 | SHIPPED (2026-08-03, `claude/loop-212` + PR #369 — one code commit plus two follow-ups forced by a Vercel schema rejection and a verifier finding, all inside the one PR, merged by this run after verifier **PASS 12/12 tier HIGH on attempt 2** — attempt 1 FAILED one criterion, see below) — markdown twins generated in the SAME generator run as the HTML (parity structural, not disciplined) + path-aware `Accept: text/markdown` negotiation for `/tokens/`, `/chains/` and their `/ko/` twins + **the silent generic fallback removed**: the `/` → `llms.txt` rewrite and its header rule now cover all 17 members of `home.html`'s `ANALYTICS_PARAMS`/`PLANNER_PARAMS` union — 16 via `missing`, `app` via a positive shadow/override rule because **Vercel hard-caps `has`/`missing` at 16 entries** (found only by deploying: the PR's first deploy was rejected by Vercel's schema at 17, every local gate green) — with tested exact set-equality against the router arrays, so no parameterized URL is ever handed the site index again. Attempt 1's verifier FAIL was real and load-bearing: `/?app=1` (the planner header's live analytics link, `planner.js:3863`) still returned `llms.txt` because the drift guard scanned `.get()` calls and the router reads `app` via `ANALYTICS_PARAMS.some(k => params.has(k))` — guard rebuilt around the router arrays themselves. | 244 | **P0, first on record: the analytics grid boots with `React is not defined` and renders ZERO pool cards on the sacred `?token=` surface.** Audit `2026-08-06T07:15:06.317Z` (83 surfaces) flagged two findings on one surface — `[P0] grid-token :: page-error` (`pageerror: React is not defined` + `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'some') at getPoolTypeShared (PoolDetail.compiled.min.js:1:1510)`) and `[P1] grid-token :: dead-end` (`valid grid query rendered no .pool-card within 10s`). **Mechanism confirmed from the error signature, not guessed:** `PoolDetail.compiled.min.js` line 1 is ONE comma-separated `var` statement — `var{useState,useEffect}=React,LENDING_PROTOCOLS=[…],…,RWA_PROTOCOLS=[…]`; if `React` is undefined the destructuring throws so every later declarator in that statement never assigns, while the hoisted `function getPoolTypeShared` stays callable and reads `RWA_PROTOCOLS.some(…)` first — which is *exactly* the observed error pair, in the observed order. **Why React can be missing:** `home.html:178-179` load React/ReactDOM with `defer`; `home.html:350-364` appends `PoolDetail.compiled.min.js` via `document.body.appendChild` from an inline script at end-of-body — dynamically inserted scripts are **async by default** and nothing orders them against the deferred tags. The `onload` chain that file's own comment (`:343-347`) describes orders only the two compiled bundles against *each other*; `onload` fires even on a script that threw, so `app.compiled.min.js` loads on top of the wreckage. **Rate, both numbers:** 1/1 in the contended 83-surface run, **0/6** isolated `runAudit({only:['grid-token']})` repeats (which showed grid-token's usual 2 occlusion findings instead) — contention-dependent, not reproducible on demand, and NOT dismissible: the missing barrier is structural in the same `home.html` prod serves. Prod rate is **unknown and presently unmeasurable** — every candidate witness is blind or unexercised (`error_occurred` = 0 for 24d over ~0 real analytics-mode human loads; §2a prediction filed in weakest form). **Weakest predicate (RAZOR):** the class is not "React for PoolDetail" but *every dynamically-inserted script reading a global supplied by a `defer` tag* — enumerate the deferred globals and their consumers FROM the files (212's mirror rule), never a hand-typed list. | 9.0 | IN_REVIEW→SHIPPED on merge (2026-08-06, ONE commit on `claude/inspiring-mendel-ehuajl` + PR, 1 attempt). | 268 | **The shipped x402 surface targets a superseded protocol version and a payload shape the protocol does not define — no real x402 client or facilitator can transact with it.** Item 234 merged 2026-08-11 (`dc9892a45b`, PR #432, verifier PASS 7/7) and is honest about a long residue list in `edge/X402.md`; these three are NOT on it, and were found by reading the standard raw (`x402-foundation/x402`, the Linux Foundation project that owns it) rather than the announcement blog. **(1) It implements x402 v1; v2 has been the standard since 2025-12-11.** `edge/X402.md:69,94` publish `"version": 1`; `edge/x402-core.js:322,328,360` emit `x402Version:1` + `maxAmountRequired` and read `X-PAYMENT`. V2 moved the challenge into a base64 `PAYMENT-REQUIRED` header, renamed the client header to `PAYMENT-SIGNATURE`, renamed `maxAmountRequired`->`amount`, made `network` CAIP-2 and `resource` a `ResourceInfo` object. V1 is still SDK-supported, so this alone is a should-fix, not a P1. **(2) THE LOAD-BEARING ONE: the documented payment payload `{x402Version, scheme, network, resource, amount, payer?}` (`edge/X402.md` "How to pay" step 2) matches NEITHER version.** v1 §5.2 and v2 §5.2 both require a `payload` object carrying an EIP-3009 `signature` + `authorization`; the shipped shape has no signature at all and a top-level `amount` neither version defines there. Consequences: no reference-SDK client can pay this API, and a facilitator's `/verify` (which takes the SPEC `paymentPayload` type) would reject our POST on schema — so **`X402_MODE=live` can never succeed even after the human completes every human-owned leg**. The gate fails closed, so this is not a leak; it is a paid path that cannot transact. **(3) `/mcp` answers with the HTTP 402 shape, but x402 defines an MCP transport** (`specs/transports-v2/mcp.md`): a paid tool without payment MUST return a JSON-RPC *result* (HTTP 200) with `isError:true` and `PaymentRequired` in BOTH `structuredContent` and `content[0].text`, with payment in `params._meta["x402/payment"]`. `edge/X402.md` states the HTTP-shape choice deliberately with a pricing rationale, but that answers a pricing question, not a signalling one — Cloudflare's own `withX402Client` (from the announcement this pillar is built on) will not find requirements where we put them, and an HTTP 402 out of a JSON-RPC endpoint breaks plain MCP clients too. Fix: emit v2 + accept both request headers, validate the real `PaymentPayload`, use the MCP transport shape, and guard the CLASS with a version constant checked against a committed spec fixture (catches a version bump; NOT arbitrary within-version drift — stated honestly). Settlement stays out of scope (234's residue decision stands). Nothing becomes chargeable; the gate stays dark | 7.6 | READY | HIGH (public agent surface + protocol code + the `/api` and `/mcp` response shapes; no trust rail touched, nothing becomes chargeable) | specs/268.md | 0 | — |

(1) **Verdict semantics**: BLOCKING (only a code-behavior change fixes it; consumes an attempt) vs AMEND (a claim corrected / residue disclosed with a number; same round, ships, consumes nothing), plus the oscillation rule — a BLOCKING finding introduced by the previous round's fix parks immediately. Calibration: 228 ships round 1, 263 still parks. (2) **Ceremony caps**, over-cap = verifier FAIL category `ceremony` resolved by DELETING text: spec ≤80/150 · notes ≤60/200 · PR ≤40/100+quiz · LOG ≤300 chars · non-vacuity 1 per gate file (LOW) / per rule (HIGH) · verifier rounds 1/2 · test:code ≤2:1/≤3:1. (3) **Pickup arithmetic**: a process item is picked only when it beats the best READY product item by >1.0, max ONE in flight — backtest 263-process (8.0) beat 265-product (7.8) by 0.2 and the day shipped zero product impact. (4) **Wall clock**: T+10 dispatched · T+75 builder deadline (over → descope) · T+95 verifier round · T+120 merged or parked; terminal states MERGED / PARKED-with-recipe / SKIPPED (<20k tokens) only. (5) **Impact + vitals**: every spec carries `Impact: <north-star leg | guardrail | defect-count n→0 on <surface>>` repeated verbatim as the PR body's first line (cannot fill it → process item by definition), and every heartbeat prints one fixed-format vitals line with breach thresholds (parked PRs >2, process share >25%, tokens/ship >600k) filing at most ONE triage item.
